The line: (4,2) -> (3,3) -> (2,4) -> (1,5)

Yes, that's a diagonal. The knight is pinned.
a2a3 attacks the bishop.
b2b3 attacks the bishop.
f1b5 attacks the bishop.

Between a2a3 and b2b3:
a2a3 keeps the b-pawn to protect the knight (if the bishop takes, we take with b2).
b2b3 moves the protector of c3.
So a2a3 is better than b2b3.

What about f1b5?
If f1b5:
If ...Bxb5, then we can't take back with the knight (pinned).
We can't take back with the pawn (b2).
We have nothing to take back with.
So we just lost a bishop for nothing.
So f1b5 is a blunder.

Therefore, a2a3 is the best move.
